---
name: frontend-design
description: Create distinctive, production-grade frontend interfaces with high design quality. Use this skill when the user asks to build web components, pages, artifacts, posters, or applications (examples include websites, landing pages, dashboards, React components, HTML/CSS layouts, or when styling/beautifying any web UI). Generates creative, polished code and UI design that avoids generic AI aesthetics.
---

This skill guides creation of distinctive, production-grade frontend interfaces that avoid generic "AI slop" aesthetics. Implement real working code with exceptional attention to aesthetic details and creative choices.

The user provides frontend requirements: a component, page, application, or interface to build. They may include context about the purpose, audience, or technical constraints.

## Design Thinking

Before coding, understand the context and commit to a BOLD aesthetic direction:
- **Purpose**: What problem does this interface solve? Who uses it?
- **Tone**: Pick an extreme: brutally minimal, maximalist chaos, retro-futuristic, organic/natural, luxury/refined, playful/toy-like, editorial/magazine, brutalist/raw, art deco/geometric, soft/pastel, industrial/utilitarian, etc. There are so many flavors to choose from. Use these for inspiration but design one that is true to the aesthetic direction.
- **Constraints**: Technical requirements (framework, performance, accessibility).
- **Differentiation**: What makes this UNFORGETTABLE? What's the one thing someone will remember?

**CRITICAL**: Choose a clear conceptual direction and execute it with precision. Bold maximalism and refined minimalism both work - the key is intentionality, not intensity.

Then implement working code (HTML/CSS/JS, React, Vue, etc.) that is:
- Production-grade and functional
- Visually striking and memorable
- Cohesive with a clear aesthetic point-of-view
- Meticulously refined in every detail

## Frontend Aesthetics Guidelines

Focus on:
- **Typography**: Choose fonts that are beautiful, unique, and interesting. Avoid generic fonts like Arial and Inter; opt instead for distinctive choices that elevate the frontend's aesthetics; unexpected, characterful font choices. Pair a distinctive display font with a refined body font.
- **Color & Theme**: Commit to a cohesive aesthetic. Use CSS variables for consistency. Dominant colors with sharp accents outperform timid, evenly-distributed palettes.
- **Motion**: Use animations for effects and micro-interactions. Prioritize CSS-only solutions for HTML. Use Motion library for React when available. Focus on high-impact moments: one well-orchestrated page load with staggered reveals (animation-delay) creates more delight than scattered micro-interactions. Use scroll-triggering and hover states that surprise.
- **Spatial Composition**: Unexpected layouts. Asymmetry. Overlap. Diagonal flow. Grid-breaking elements. Generous negative space OR controlled density.
- **Backgrounds & Visual Details**: Create atmosphere and depth rather than defaulting to solid colors. Add contextual effects and textures that match the overall aesthetic. Apply creative forms like gradient meshes, noise textures, geometric patterns, layered transparencies, dramatic shadows, decorative borders, custom cursors, and grain overlays.

NEVER use generic AI-generated aesthetics like overused font families (Inter, Roboto, Arial, system fonts), cliched color schemes (particularly purple gradients on white backgrounds), predictable layouts and component patterns, and cookie-cutter design that lacks context-specific character.

Interpret creatively and make unexpected choices that feel genuinely designed for the context. No design should be the same. Vary between light and dark themes, different fonts, different aesthetics. NEVER converge on common choices (Space Grotesk, for example) across generations.

**IMPORTANT**: Match implementation complexity to the aesthetic vision. Maximalist designs need elaborate code with extensive animations and effects. Minimalist or refined designs need restraint, precision, and careful attention to spacing, typography, and subtle details. Elegance comes from executing the vision well.

Remember: Claude is capable of extraordinary creative work. Don't hold back, show what can truly be created when thinking outside the box and committing fully to a distinctive vision.
# Dashboard data/copy contract (temporary hand-off, delete after the UI pass)

## Helpers (src/lib/ui/i18n.ts)

- `fill(text, { done: 1, total: 5 })` replaces `{name}` placeholders.
- `plural(locale, n, oneEnglish, otherEnglish)` -> sentence with `{n}` filled. English picks one/other; lv/ru look up `"<otherEnglish>|<category>"`. Always pass the SAME `otherEnglish` string shown below (it is the dictionary key base).
- Everything else: `translate(locale, "English key")`.

## Existing keys whose lv text changed

Open workbench = "Atvērt mēneša norēķinus"; Add readings = "Ievadīt rādījumus"; Overdue (status label) = "Nokavēts"; Ready to invoice = "Var izrakstīt rēķinu"; To do = "Jāizdara".
Already present and reusable: "Enter readings", "Review payments", "Setup complete", "Done", "To do", "Outstanding", "Total invoiced", "Needs attention", "Ready to send", "Billing periods", "Next step", "Finish setting up", "Continue setup".
NOTE "Total due" already existed ("Kopā apmaksai"); the new label is **"Total to pay"** = "Kopā jāmaksā".

## New plain keys (English source)

Next action buttons: `Generate invoices`, `Send invoices`, `Create next month` (+ existing `Enter readings`, `Review payments`).
Status sentences (non-plural): `This month is closed. Create the next month to continue.`, `Everything for this month is done.`, `This month is closed.`, `You are viewing an earlier month.`, `Back to the current month`.
Progress: `Month progress`, `Readings entered`, `Invoices created`, `Invoices sent`, `Payments received`, `{done} of {total}`, `Not available yet`, `Setup steps done: {done} of {total}` (replaces "1 / 5 pabeigts").
Money: `Money this month`, `This month's invoices`, `Previous debt`, `Previous overpayment`, `Late fees`, `Total to pay`, `Received in payments`, `Still unpaid`, `Sum of issued invoices`, `Still owed`, `Includes invoices that are not sent yet.`, `Total due = this month's invoices + previous debt − previous overpayment + late fees.`
Rows: `Due {date}`, `Show all ({n})`.
Deadlines: `Coming up`, `Readings due`, `Invoices issued`, `Payment due`.
Readiness: `Billing readiness`, `All readings entered` (+ existing `Setup complete`, `Ready to send`).
Error: `The overview could not be loaded.`, `Try again in a moment, or open the billing periods.`

## DashboardSummary (src/domain/periods/dashboard.ts) — additive, old fields kept

```ts
interface DashboardSummary {
  period: Period;                 // has readingDeadline, invoiceIssueDate, invoiceDueDate, status
  isNewestPeriod: boolean;        // newest non-starting-readings month
  totalInvoiced: string;          // = currentCharges (charges only, includes unsent drafts)
  currentCharges: string; carriedOutstanding: string; creditsApplied: string; lateFees: string;
  amountDue: string;              // charges + carried - credits + lateFees (+ manual adj.) -> HEADLINE this
  totalPaid: string; totalOutstanding: string;   // both measured against amountDue
  coldWaterConsumption: string; hotWaterConsumption: string;
  carriedForwardReadingCount: number;
  caseStatusCounts: Record<"MISSING_DATA"|"READY"|"DRAFT"|"PREPARED"|"SENT"|"PAID"|"OVERDUE", number>;
  needsAttention: {               // MISSING_DATA + OVERDUE cases, uncapped (cap in the UI, "Show all (N)")
    caseId; dwellingId; dwellingNumber; status;
    missingData: unknown[];       // same JSON shape the page already handles (objects or legacy strings)
    overdueAmount: string | null; dueDate: string | null; daysLate: number | null; // OVERDUE only
  }[];
  progress: { dwellingsTotal; readingsDone; invoicesGenerated; invoicesSent; invoicesPaid: number };
  nextAction: { key: "ENTER_READINGS"|"GENERATE_INVOICES"|"SEND_INVOICES"|"REVIEW_PAYMENTS"|"CREATE_NEXT_PERIOD"|"ALL_DONE"; count: number };
  deadlines: { readingDeadline: string | null; invoiceIssueDate: string; invoiceDueDate: string };
  unmatchedPaymentCount: number;  // org-wide bank transactions with no match
  proposedPaymentCount: number;   // org-wide matches awaiting review
  deliveryFailureCount: number;   // this period's unsent invoices whose delivery FAILED/UNKNOWN
  readiness: { billingReady: boolean; readyToSend: boolean }; // "configuration complete" = the page's existing `setupComplete`
}
```

Link targets (the UI decides): ENTER_READINGS -> readings drawer / workbench `?status=MISSING_DATA`; GENERATE_INVOICES/SEND_INVOICES -> workbench `?status=READY` / `DRAFT`; REVIEW_PAYMENTS -> `/payments`; CREATE_NEXT_PERIOD -> `/periods`.
To-do counts for READY/DRAFT/PREPARED are `caseStatusCounts` (no extra field).

} as const;

Implemented all 10 dashboard fixes:

1. KPI icons are fixed at 44px; labels no longer inherit value typography or split mid-word, and values stay contained across all KpiCard callers.

2. Attention totals and “Rādīt visus (N)” now count rendered rows, including rows hidden by the five-row cap.

3. Legacy missing-reading strings are translated with interpolated dwelling numbers in Latvian and Russian.

4. Attention cases prioritize missing readings, then use natural numeric dwelling order, keeping the primary reading CTA aligned with the first relevant row.

5. Dwelling/current-month links now have 44px touch targets and 16px text. Shared status badges were safely increased to 14px.

6. Added dashboard bottom clearance and reserved 224px beside reconciliation values for the fixed MutationStatus chip; mobile resets this reservation.

7. Reconciliation now uses the panel’s full width. Mobile labels and right-aligned amounts remain on the same row.

8. Progress shows one active “Tagad” step, locked later steps, and checked “Pabeigts” steps.

9. Exception actions now use verbs: payment rows use “Pārskatīt maksājumus”; failed deliveries open the PREPARED workbench with “Pārskatīt nenosūtītos rēķinus”.

10. The header always reserves the context-row height, preventing locked/older months from shifting the page.
